I've also been ignoring calls from my friends and my ex-wife, and I can't even bring myself to go to my son's games anymore. It has really strained my relationships and made it incredibly hard to manage daily life.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices the only unusual experience you have, or do you also feel that people are specifically targeting you, plotting against you, or that you have special abilities or secret knowledge?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely feel targeted. I've been hearing these clear voices steadily for about four months now, though my overall struggles first started about 14 months ago after a security incident at work.\n\nDoctor: During these past 14 months, have you also experienced significant periods where your mood was extremely low, or conversely, unusually high and energetic?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any extreme highs or times when I felt unusually energetic. I've also been ignoring calls from my friends and my ex-wife, and I can't even bring myself to go to my son's games anymore. As for my emotions, while I definitely lack the drive to do much of anything, I don't feel completely expressionless or emotionally flat.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the voices and your concerns about being watched, have you experienced any other unusual perceptions, such as seeing things that aren't there or feeling sensations on your skin that have no physical cause?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't seen anything unusual or felt any strange sensations on my skin. For me, it is really just the voices I hear and that constant, overwhelming feeling of being watched and targeted.\n\nDoctor: When you talk to others, do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or that you jump between unrelated topics, making it hard for people to understand your point?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel like my thoughts are jumbled or that I'm jumping between topics when I talk. But I do know that my colleagues and family have found some of my recent recommendations and behavior pretty confusing lately.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you experienced a persistent loss of motivation, social withdrawal, or a lack of emotional expression that was present even when you weren't feeling particularly overwhelmed by the voices and the belief that you were being watched?"

It has really strained my relationships and made it incredibly hard to manage daily life.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices the only unusual experience you have, or do you also feel that people are specifically targeting you, plotting against you, or that you have special abilities or secret knowledge?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely feel targeted. I’m convinced there’s a coordinated group, maybe even within my own department, watching me through cameras and phones, waiting for me to slip up so they can pin a bigger disaster on me. I believe the news criticism from that community event was just a cover story to set this all up.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and your beliefs about being watched, have you noticed that your speech has become disorganized or difficult for others to follow, or do you find yourself feeling completely emotionally flat or expressionless?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed my speech being difficult to follow, though I suppose I'm not entirely sure how it sounds to others lately. As for my emotions, while I definitely lack the drive to do much of anything, I don't feel completely expressionless or emotionally flat.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the voices and your concerns about being watched, have you experienced any other unusual perceptions, such as seeing things that aren't there or feeling sensations on your skin that have no physical cause?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't seen anything unusual or felt any strange sensations on my skin. For me, it is really just the voices I hear and that constant, overwhelming feeling of being watched and targeted.\n\nDoctor: When you talk to others, do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or that you jump between unrelated topics, making it hard for people to understand your point?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel like my thoughts are jumbled or that I'm jumping between topics when I talk. But I do know that my colleagues and family have found some of my recent recommendations and behavior pretty confusing lately.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you experienced a persistent loss of motivation, social withdrawal, or a lack of emotional expression that was present even when you weren't feeling particularly overwhelmed by the voices and the belief that you were being watched?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when the voices are quiet, I've felt this persistent emptiness and a severe lack of drive for over a year now. I mostly just sit on the couch staring at the TV while the dishes and laundry pile up, ignoring calls from my friends and ex-wife. I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like reading about security work or going to my son's games.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, such as your thoughts feeling like they are being broadcast out loud, or a sense that your own thoughts or actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like my thoughts were being broadcast out loud or that some outside force was controlling my mind or actions. It's really just the voices and the constant feeling of being watched that I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulty in managing daily chores and social withdrawal began at the same time the voices started, or did you notice these changes in your motivation and social life even before the voices began?"
  }
]

The drop in my motivation, withdrawing from my family, and neglecting chores actually started much earlier, about a year and two months ago right after that community event incident. The voices and the feeling that I'm being targeted only started more recently, about four months ago.

I've been hearing these clear voices steadily for about four months now, though my overall struggles first started about 14 months ago after a security incident at work.\n\nDoctor: During these past 14 months, have you also experienced significant periods where your mood was extremely low, or conversely, unusually high and energetic?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced any extreme highs or times when I felt unusually energetic. I definitely feel down and have lost my motivation because of everything going on, but I wouldn't describe it as a distinct period of deep depression. It's more just feeling drained and overwhelmed by my situation.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices, have you noticed a significant decline in your ability to take care of yourself, express emotions, or engage in social interactions over the past year?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've completely lost my drive and mostly just sit on the couch staring at the TV, letting basic chores like laundry and bills pile up. I've also been ignoring calls from my friends and my ex-wife, and I can't even bring myself to go to my son's games anymore. It has really strained my relationships and made it incredibly hard to manage daily life.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices the only unusual experience you have, or do you also feel that people are specifically targeting you, plotting against you, or that you have special abilities or secret knowledge?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely feel targeted. I’m convinced there’s a coordinated group, maybe even within my own department, watching me through cameras and phones, waiting for me to slip up so they can pin a bigger disaster on me. I believe the news criticism from that community event was just a cover story to set this all up.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and your beliefs about being watched, have you noticed that your speech has become disorganized or difficult for others to follow, or do you find yourself feeling completely emotionally flat or expressionless?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed my speech being difficult to follow, though I suppose I'm not entirely sure how it sounds to others lately. As for my emotions, while I definitely lack the drive to do much of anything, I don't feel completely expressionless or emotionally flat.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the voices and your concerns about being watched, have you experienced any other unusual perceptions, such as seeing things that aren't there or feeling sensations on your skin that have no physical cause?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't seen anything unusual or felt any strange sensations on my skin. For me, it is really just the voices I hear and that constant, overwhelming feeling of being watched and targeted.\n\nDoctor: When you talk to others, do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or that you jump between unrelated topics, making it hard for people to understand your point?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel like my thoughts are jumbled or that I'm jumping between topics when I talk. But I do know that my colleagues and family have found some of my recent recommendations and behavior pretty confusing lately.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you experienced a persistent loss of motivation, social withdrawal, or a lack of emotional expression that was present even when you weren't feeling particularly overwhelmed by the voices and the belief that you were being watched?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when the voices are quiet, I've felt this persistent emptiness and a severe lack of drive for over a year now. I mostly just sit on the couch staring at the TV while the dishes and laundry pile up, ignoring calls from my friends and ex-wife. I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like reading about security work or going to my son's games.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, such as your thoughts feeling like they are being broadcast out loud, or a sense that your own thoughts or actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like my thoughts were being broadcast out loud or that some outside force was controlling my mind or actions. It's really just the voices and the constant feeling of being watched that I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulty in managing daily chores and social withdrawal began at the same time the voices started, or did you notice these changes in your motivation and social life even before the voices began?\n\nPatient: The drop in my motivation, withdrawing from my family, and neglecting chores actually started much earlier, about a year and two months ago right after that community event incident. The voices and the feeling that I'm being targeted only started more recently, about four months ago.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any other unusual beliefs or odd behaviors during those first ten months, before the voices and the feeling of being watched began?\n\nPatient: Yes, I was definitely acting oddly during that time. I became obsessively preoccupied with security risks far beyond what was realistic, working excessive hours and pacing my apartment for hours with my radio scanner. I even had some alarming behavior during a safety drill at work, which is what eventually led to them placing me on leave.\n\nDoctor: During those first ten months, did you experience a significant decline in your ability to express emotions, or did you find yourself becoming increasingly withdrawn and indifferent to social interactions even before the paranoid beliefs began?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure or don't recall experiencing a loss of emotion or social withdrawal during those first ten months. Back then, I was mostly just highly anxious and completely obsessed with revising our work protocols."

It has really strained my relationships and made it incredibly hard to manage daily life.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices the only unusual experience you have, or do you also feel that people are specifically targeting you, plotting against you, or that you have special abilities or secret knowledge?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely feel targeted. I’m convinced there’s a coordinated group, maybe even within my own department, watching me through cameras and phones, waiting for me to slip up so they can pin a bigger disaster on me. I believe the news criticism from that community event was just a cover story to set this all up.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and your beliefs about being watched, have you noticed that your speech has become disorganized or difficult for others to follow, or do you find yourself feeling completely emotionally flat or expressionless?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed my speech being difficult to follow, though I suppose I'm not entirely sure how it sounds to others lately. As for my emotions, while I definitely lack the drive to do much of anything, I don't feel completely expressionless or emotionally flat.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the voices and your concerns about being watched, have you experienced any other unusual perceptions, such as seeing things that aren't there or feeling sensations on your skin that have no physical cause?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't seen anything unusual or felt any strange sensations on my skin. For me, it is really just the voices I hear and that constant, overwhelming feeling of being watched and targeted.\n\nDoctor: When you talk to others, do you find that your thoughts feel jumbled or that you jump between unrelated topics, making it hard for people to understand your point?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel like my thoughts are jumbled or that I'm jumping between topics when I talk. But I do know that my colleagues and family have found some of my recent recommendations and behavior pretty confusing lately.\n\nDoctor: Over the past 14 months, have you experienced a persistent loss of motivation, social withdrawal, or a lack of emotional expression that was present even when you weren't feeling particularly overwhelmed by the voices and the belief that you were being watched?\n\nPatient: Yes, even when the voices are quiet, I've felt this persistent emptiness and a severe lack of drive for over a year now. I mostly just sit on the couch staring at the TV while the dishes and laundry pile up, ignoring calls from my friends and ex-wife. I've completely lost interest in things I used to love, like reading about security work or going to my son's games.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes, such as your thoughts feeling like they are being broadcast out loud, or a sense that your own thoughts or actions are being controlled by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like my thoughts were being broadcast out loud or that some outside force was controlling my mind or actions. It's really just the voices and the constant feeling of being watched that I'm dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulty in managing daily chores and social withdrawal began at the same time the voices started, or did you notice these changes in your motivation and social life even before the voices began?\n\nPatient: The drop in my motivation, withdrawing from my family, and neglecting chores actually started much earlier, about a year and two months ago right after that community event incident. The voices and the feeling that I'm being targeted only started more recently, about four months ago.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any other unusual beliefs or odd behaviors during those first ten months, before the voices and the feeling of being watched began?\n\nPatient: Yes, I was definitely acting oddly during that time. I became obsessively preoccupied with security risks far beyond what was realistic, working excessive hours and pacing my apartment for hours with my radio scanner. I even had some alarming behavior during a safety drill at work, which is what eventually led to them placing me on leave.\n\nDoctor: During those first ten months, did you experience a significant decline in your ability to express emotions, or did you find yourself becoming increasingly withdrawn and indifferent to social interactions even before the paranoid beliefs began?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure or don't recall experiencing a loss of emotion or social withdrawal during those first ten months. Back then, I was mostly just highly anxious and completely obsessed with revising our work protocols.\n\nDoctor: During those first ten months, did you notice that you were becoming less animated, less expressive in your facial gestures, or that you were having more difficulty maintaining a normal conversation than you had in the past?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I don't recall noticing those specific changes during that early period. I don't think I had less facial expression or any real trouble keeping up a normal conversation back then."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
